Compiler Timings
================

How long the compiler takes on each sample program, in milliseconds, the
best of five runs.  One row per commit and one column per program: the
commits go on for ever, so the tables grow down rather than across, and
the programs are split into groups by what would move them.  A blank
means the sample did not exist yet at that commit.

A table is at most fifteen columns wide, counting the one that names the
commit; a group with more programs than that is written as several,
numbered.  Samples are never removed, so every group reaches that width
in the end -- and a column nobody can see beside the commit it belongs to
is a column nobody can read.

Measured on one machine, so the numbers are worth comparing with each
other and with nobody else's.  A hash ending in `+` was measured with
changes not yet committed.  Written by `bin/pl4g-timing`.

Work
----

The sum of the compiler's own stages, which is what a change to the
compiler moves.

### The smallest programs

What a compilation costs when there is next to nothing to compile.  These move when the driver, the lexer or
the parser does, and they are the floor everything else is measured against.

-------

for.  For the bootstrap compiler this is mostly starting the

what the next costs is noise; only the range across all of them is

What each row is:

- `eee64a3` -- ✨ A register allocator, by linear scan, which does not spill
- `af657ad` -- ✨ Conditional branches, selected with their comparison and turned round
- `2f564cb` -- ✨ Expressions, by precedence climbing, with the bitwise operators
- `37dbb28` -- 🐛 A diagnostic number no block covers is refused, not mis-grouped
- `1c4ae78` -- ✨ What the compiler leaves out is recorded in the decision log
- `f391839` -- ✨ A local the sweep removes is logged too, by the name it was given
- `04c20c9` -- ✨ A grammar for editors, and a program that shows a log against its source
- `3020cf6` -- ✨ Spilling to a stack frame, by rewriting and starting again
- `9bcaf84` -- ⚡ A spilled value is read from the frame once for as many instructions as read it
- `40c35bb` -- 📝 Timing tables grow down, not across, and are grouped
- `9437d8a` -- ✨ Modules: read while compiling, read once, named by the shortest route
- `d0c8cf5` -- ✨ `export` and `visible` are two attributes, because they were two questions
- `73ce857` -- ✨ One meaning, one spelling: attributes are written one way
- `ed1c028` -- ✨ A truth value in a register, on all three architectures
- `1269bd5` -- ✨ The comparisons: `=` `≠` `<` `>` `≤` `≥`
- `db0b436` -- ✨ A statement's value must be used, and ≠ keeps its glyph
- `25bb4e0` -- ✨ The logical operators, and block parameters to carry `and` and `or`
- `baf1f3c` -- ✨ Constants of any width, and the header flags they travel under
- `74ac227` -- ✨ Saturating arithmetic: ⊞ ⊟ ⊠
- `c0f29f2` -- ✨ Arithmetic that checks, and the path a fault leaves through
- `5c3aec4` -- ✨ No arrow means nothing answered with; a semicolon separates and never ends
- `f7ba2fc` -- ✅ A call that answers with nothing has nothing to use
- `3e9eb27` -- ✨ Function calls, with positional arguments
- `0990c74` -- ✨ Division and what is left over: `÷` and `%`
- `05d8da1` -- ✨ Moving bits sideways: « » ↺ ↻, and an overflow seen while compiling
- `03887ff` -- 📝 Time the floating-point sample too
- `87c066b` -- ✨ A floating-point answer that is not a number stops the program
- `ca395a6` -- 📝 Time the approximate comparisons too
- `230b8a7` -- 📝 Time the result type too
- `5706c5f` -- 📝 Time the type definitions too
- `41f13c3` -- 📝 Time a result passed to a function too
- `e7fb804` -- 📝 Time a match too
- `d0caa00` -- 📝 Time the enumerations too
- `df0bfc5` -- 📝 Time a flag enumeration too
- `af86f5d` -- 📝 Time an if too
- `7de9bb1` -- 📝 Time the collections too
- `08bdd97` -- 📝 The collections are not a timing sample yet
- `3ef3db4` -- 📝 Timings for the collections front end
- `90846b5` -- 📝 Take the collection sample out of the timings for good
- `e927edf` -- 📝 Time the tuples too
- `53908ab` -- ✨ Reach a place through an address held in a register
- `9ffbf29` -- ✨ An allocator: a bump pointer over mapped chunks
- `603f175` -- ✨ A branch backwards: liveness over the graph, and a parallel copy
- `f067535` -- ✨ `while`
- `bbf4758` -- ✨ `foreach`, iterators and ranges
- `c374354` -- ✨ Sets and dictionaries, and the arena they live in
- `9e55aab` -- ✨ A calling convention per function, and `@[cdecl]` for the system's
- `9958f2d` -- ⚡ Work out every physical register's stretches in one walk
- `bed0605` -- ✨ Arrays, fixed and dynamic
- `beb9413` -- ✨ An array has a shape
- `e29a5db` -- ✨ A loop takes its values from four things, not one
- `659fce3` -- ✨ A member of a tuple is named the way an element of an array is
- `fa870d4` -- ✨ A tuple's index is every constant the compiler knows, and only those
- `b3271e5` -- ✨ A parameter a function may change
- `decaf2f` -- ✨ A tuple handed over as several arguments
- `666d4b2` -- ✨ An array spread, and a spread among a tuple's members
- `b357677` -- 🐛 An array written down is lowered once, and never with no builder
- `415f40d` -- ✨ A loop with a name, left early or begun again
- `93585e6` -- ✨ Everything is worked out left to right, and once
- `a4654dc` -- ✨ What a loop comes to
- `a9a5996` -- 🐛 What is wanted travels down to what was written
- `94d8a22` -- ✨ Answering with more than the registers hold
- `208d046` -- ✨ An answer that has to be taken, and `_` to drop one
- `a86ce70` -- ✨ A function changes nothing unless it says it may
- `037c8c8` -- 🔥 A scratch binary that was never meant to be committed
- `ce9a789` -- 🐛 A call the program did not want goes at every level
- `df6ee64` -- 🔥 A scratch log file, and a root that says what belongs in it
- `1280755` -- 🐛 The mark in a decision stands over the column it means
- `a3391c9` -- ✨ An array walked where one of its elements is wanted
- `b289549` -- ✨ The operators walk arrays
- `9471953` -- ✨ x86-64 microarchitecture levels, checked at startup
- `6b67cc7` -- ✨ Picking an array with a mask
- `69b1e48` -- ♻️ Annotations are read, not evaluated
- `b2f65e7` -- ♻️ A program the runtime stops exits, and 64 through 127 are its own
- `c091086` -- ⚡ Narrow arithmetic is done narrow, and the flags say whether it fit
- `de02cdf` -- 📝 What an operator over a whole run means, and what it does not
- `2b9fd68` -- ✨ ⎕wrap, which says of a region that its arithmetic may go past the end
- `914edae` -- ♻️ A saturating operator inside ⎕wrap is an error
- `7bc2fd8` -- ✨ ⧺, which joins two arrays
- `4f71dab` -- ✨ char, which is one code point and not a number
- `f5bbfe4` -- ✨ str, which is UTF-8 and is walked rather than indexed
- `2611c08` -- ✨ #, which answers how many
- `d364c4f` -- ✨ ⍴, which answers a shape and makes something of one
- `ccfd995` -- ✨ Lists, whose elements will not have to agree and for now must
- `701ddf3` -- ♻️ A foreach is written the way a let is, colon and all
- `7539871` -- ✨ comptime, and a type that is never a value
- `49c4603` -- ✨ ⌈ and ⌊: the larger of two, and the largest of several
- `8b31221` -- ✨ Characters and strings compare, and strings compare by their bytes
- `e8f3eee` -- ✨ Four rounding operators, written with arrows
- `89e5113` -- ✨ RISC-V is built for an ISA string or a profile, and rva23 by default
- `891d3e2` -- ✨ A RISC-V image says what it was built for, and rv32 is not this target
- `d739a9e` -- ✨ Raising to a power, written ⁿ and written raised
- `1a89b68` -- ✨ A power answers a result, and a negative exponent is the division it means
- `3e569b2` -- 🐛 A floating-point comparison is not absorbed by the branch that reads it
- `71415cf` -- ✨ Integer types of any width, and a status that is a u6
- `1e6859a` -- ✨ Lifting, written ⌜x⌝, and the ends of a type
- `485a100` -- ✨ ⎕enumerate, which counts the turns of any walk
- `c328593` -- ✨ ∣ and ∤: dividing without a remainder, and what zero divides
- `cd6d911` -- ✨ ∣ answers a result where its divisor is not written down

What each program exercises:

- `exit0` -- the smallest conforming program
- `semicolon-separates-statements` -- two statements on one line
- `digit-separators` -- literals in every base
- `boolean-values` -- truth values in both sections
- `character-values` -- code points written eight ways, and both conversions
- `string-walk` -- a walk over every length a UTF-8 sequence has
- `string-join` -- four joins, and the arena they come out of
- `string-compare` -- all six comparisons of strings, and the one walk behind them
- `character-compare` -- all six comparisons of code points
- `length-operator` -- how many, asked of all five kinds of thing
- `shape-operator` -- shapes answered and shapes made, filled three ways
- `extremum-operators` -- the largest and smallest, asked of all seven kinds of thing
- `list-values` -- lists made, walked and counted
- `list-join` -- four joins of lists, two element widths
- `global-variable` -- one variable, read once
- `assign-widths` -- a store of every width
- `boolean-in-memory` -- a truth value, which is one byte
- `many-values-at-once` -- four values live at once
- `spill-to-the-frame` -- thirty-two at once, which no target can hold
- `spill-around-a-loop` -- thirty at once across a loop, which is weighed
- `unreached-function` -- a function and a variable that are dropped
- `export-visibility` -- several definitions, some exported
- `bitwise-operators` -- four operators over two variables
- `bitwise-precedence` -- an expression the folder collapses entirely
- `comparison-operators` -- all six comparisons, none of them folded
- `logic-operators` -- all six logical operators, none of them folded
- `logic-short-circuit` -- three short circuits, which is six blocks
- `saturating-precedence` -- a sum and a product that saturate
- `saturating-widest-multiplication` -- six products of whole registers
- `arithmetic-operators` -- a sum and a product that check and can fault
- `arithmetic-at-each-width` -- a sum and a difference at each of the four widths
- `count-bits` -- counting bits, with the instruction where there is one
- `wrap-arithmetic` -- fifteen operators that may go past the end of their type
- `call-nested` -- four calls, two of them nested
- `assign-to-the-wildcard` -- two answers taken and two dropped into '_'
- `pure-by-default` -- four pure functions, one of them writing its own array
- `pure-call-is-dropped` -- a pure call dropped and an impure one kept
- `parameter-mut` -- a parameter counted down in, and one that is not
- `cdecl-attribute` -- two conventions in one program, called both ways
- `arithmetic-division` -- a division and a remainder, each checked twice
- `shift-operators` -- two shifts, each with its distance checked
- `float-arithmetic` -- ten floating-point answers, each asserted
- `float-approximate` -- every approximate comparison, in both widths
- `float-comparison-as-a-condition` -- floating-point comparisons where a condition stands
- `extremum-of-floats` -- the larger and smaller of floating-point values, in both widths
- `rounding-operators` -- all four roundings, in both widths, over values and an array
- `power-operator` -- powers written raised and worked out, and one over an array
- `narrow-integers` -- types of three, five, one and thirty-one bits, in every operation
- `result-type` -- a result made, propagated with ? and read with ??
- `result-as-an-argument` -- results passed and answered with, both kinds
- `result-from-a-plain-value` -- a result wanted in nine places, and no suffix written
- `match-a-result` -- three matches, one of them carrying a name past
- `if-statement` -- a chain of conditions, and one with no else
- `tuple-values` -- a tuple answered with, and two taken apart
- `tuple-element` -- members of a tuple named by where they stand
- `tuple-answered-through-storage` -- answers of three and five values, and of two
- `call-spread` -- three calls handed a tuple's members as arguments
- `order-left-to-right` -- four lists and an operator, each worked out in turn
- `call-spread-an-array` -- an array and a table spread into two calls
- `tuple-holding-a-result` -- a tuple and a record whose member is itself several values
- `tuple-spread` -- four tuples made out of others' members
- `array-written-with-no-length` -- three arrays written where no length is wanted
- `array-written-on-its-own` -- two arrays looked in where they are written
- `type-definitions` -- eight definitions in all three notations
- `enum-values` -- two enumerations, and three matches over them
- `enum-flag` -- a flag enumeration, its operators and three matches
- `array-fixed` -- an array whose type says how long it is
- `array-slice` -- a run of elements, passed to a function
- `array-global` -- an array at the top level, read three times
- `array-table` -- a table, a cube, and one of no stated shape
- `array-row` -- a row taken out of a table, and passed on
- `array-masked-pick` -- four picks, by rows and by elements
- `concatenate-arrays` -- five joins, one of them of two whole registers
- `concatenate-tables` -- tables and cubes joined along their first dimension
- `array-masked-write` -- four writes through a mask, one of them by rows
- `listable-over-an-array` -- four walks, one of them over a table twice
- `listable-operators` -- ten operators walked, and a table against a column
- `operators-over-a-run` -- runs of four lengths, on either side of a register
- `run-of-each-width` -- the bitwise three at every lane width
- `run-arithmetic` -- eight runs added and subtracted, each checked
- `run-longer-than-a-register` -- a run of two registers and one of one and a bit
- `wrap-over-a-run` -- four runs whose operators may go past, one of them multiplied
- `conditions-pre-and-post` -- a condition on the way in and one on the way out
- `requirements-bundle` -- two bundles applied, and four requirements met
- `requirements-settle-a-type` -- one requirement, whose arrow settles the answer
- `conditions-entry-value` -- a post-condition over what a parameter was at entry
- `conditions-observed` -- two conditions a build asked to observe rather than stop for
- `bundle-imported` -- a bundle from another module, which applies one of its own
- `operator-defined` -- an operator a program defined for a type of its own
- `operator-fresh` -- two operators the language has no meaning for at all
- `operator-pair` -- brackets a program defined, one of them with two indices
- `macro-rules` -- four invocations of two macros, one matching a product
- `macro-statements-and-hygiene` -- a macro writing statements, with a name renamed
- `macro-function-form` -- a macro written as a function, compiled and then run
- `macro-reflection` -- a macro asking what a piece of the program is made by
- `macro-comptime-fn` -- a function the macros call, which recurses over a tree
- `generic-imported` -- a generic function from a module, as an operator and by name
- `reflect-record` -- a nested record's text, written by reflection over its fields
- `print-and-println` -- a line written as one request, and bytes written in full
- `named-inside-a-type` -- three definitions of one name, two of them a type's
- `std-text` -- a value's text, for seven types out of one definition
- `while-loop` -- one loop, carrying two values
- `while-loop-nested` -- a loop inside a loop, in both notations
- `foreach-over-a-range` -- a loop over a range, counting up by one
- `foreach-range-step` -- three ranges, one of them counting down
- `loop-break` -- a loop left early, and what it carries handed over
- `foreach-declares-the-turn` -- six loops whose binding says what a turn is
- `comptime-tuple-loop` -- a tuple walked while compiling, and chosen by type
- `lifting` -- types lifted out of the program, and the ends of six of them
- `enumerate` -- five kinds of thing walked with their turns counted
- `divides` -- dividing without a remainder, both forms and over an array
- `failure-written-out` -- failures written out, carrying a value and not
- `named-arguments` -- defaults, arguments named at the call, and one across a module
- `references` -- references made, read, written, rebound and handed to three calls
- `units` -- units made, derived, dropped, put on and used as an index
- `one-element-says-the-type` -- seven literals whose type one entry settles
- `narrow` -- every way a value can fail to fit, and the ones that cannot
- `lambda` -- lambdas made, captured both ways, handed on and called through
- `listable-lambda` -- a lambda that walks, over a vector, a table and two arguments
- `function-as-a-value` -- four functions named where a value is wanted, one of them walking
- `generic-functions` -- one function compiled for seven sets of types
- `lifetimes` -- how long six references live, said both ways and worked out
- `named-lifetimes` -- one lifetime name over three types, and both answers
- `type-reaches-itself-through-a-reference` -- four definitions that reach themselves through references
- `line-breaks-inside-brackets` -- every kind of bracket written down the page
- `block-on-one-line` -- eight blocks written after the colon on their own line
- `tests-of-three-kinds` -- a test of each kind, and what each binary holds
- `syscall` -- two requests to the kernel, one of them with every argument
- `record-values` -- a record made, read, handed to a call and answered with
- `record-in-a-record` -- a record holding records, and a reference into one
- `answer-in-storage-not-read` -- an answer through storage that nothing reads
- `record-through-a-call` -- a record holding records handed to a call and answered with
- `record-of-one-field` -- a record of one field made, passed, answered with and referred into
- `match-on-a-sum` -- a value of a sum made, matched and taken apart
- `sum-through-a-call` -- a sum handed to a function and answered with
- `std-init` -- the descriptors a program starts with, and a write through one
- `ordered-access` -- a read that acquires and a write that releases
- `place-at-an-address` -- memory the kernel gave the program, read one value and many at a time
- `assign-a-field` -- a field of a record written, through a name and through a reference
- `record-at-the-top-level` -- a record a program starts with, read, written and referred into
- `widening` -- a value made one of a type with room for it, three ways
- `std-read-write` -- a pipe made, written through the ring and read back
- `address-of-a-place` -- a place as the number it is, and back again
- `tests-that-fail` -- three tests run, two of them named, and the status that says so
- `external-function` -- the runtime the compiler carries, reached and handed an abi record
- `narrow-to-an-enum` -- a number read as one of an enumeration's values
- `write-text` -- text written to a descriptor the program started with
- `write-and-flush` -- a write started, waited for, and one left to the drain
- `arguments` -- the words a program was named with, walked
- `fault-says-who-called` -- a fault four calls down, with a table to walk
- `stack-overflow` -- a recursion that runs off the bottom of its stack
- `loop-break-nested` -- two loops and all four jumps between them
- `loop-continue` -- two loops each begun again from inside the body
- `loop-break-value` -- a search whose loop comes to what it found
- `loop-else` -- three loops with an arm for the way that ran out
- `loop-break-value-nested` -- a search of two dimensions as one expression
- `foreach-over-an-array` -- a vector, a table by rows, and a run
- `foreach-over-a-collection` -- a set and a dictionary, walked three ways
- `collection-syntax` -- a set and a dictionary, read and written
- `collection-operators` -- all four set operators, over two sets
- `collection-grows` -- forty entries, which is four doublings
- `collection-arena` -- two arenas, and a set out of each
- `collection-string-keys` -- text as a key, and text as what it stands for
- `environment` -- the environment, which is a table the compiler builds
- `collection-take` -- keys taken out, and the entries they leave given up
- `collection-at-the-top-level` -- tables a generated constructor builds
- `list-take` -- elements taken out of a list, which moves what follows
- `list-cursor` -- a walk over a list that takes elements out as it goes
